    "content": "Core Scientific seeks $3.3 billion bond sale to further AI data center pivot Search / News Video Prices Research Consensus 2026 Data & Indices Sponsored Search / en Finance Share Share this article Copy link X icon X (Twitter) LinkedIn Facebook Email Core Scientific seeks $3.3 billion bond sale to further AI data center pivot The company is building six AI data centers, leased to CoreWeave for 12 years, and is expected to generate approximately $10 billion in revenue. By Francisco Rodrigues | Edited by Aoyon Ashraf Apr 21, 2026, 7:27 p.m. Make preferred on What to know : Core Scientific (CORZ) is raising $3.3B via a junk bond sale to finance its shift from crypto mining to AI-focused data center operations. The company is building six AI data centers, leased to CoreWeave for 12 years, and is expected to generate approximately $10 billion in revenue. This high-yield debt issuance (part of $17.9B raised in AI infrastructure this year) will fund construction costs, repay existing debt, and build reserves. Core Scientific (CORZ) is preparing to raise $3.3 billion through a junk bond sale as it continues its transition toward artificial intelligence-focused data center operations. Demand for AI services has pushed data centers, power supply and advanced chips to their limits. To keep up, firms are tapping riskier parts of the debt market for funds to keep developing their operations. Core Scientific, once a bitcoin miner, sold $175 million in bitcoin last month to further its AI pivot. Borrowers linked to AI infrastructure have raised $17.9 billion in junk bonds so far this year, Bloomberg reported . CORZ itself is building six data centers that will support AI workloads, with the capacity leased to CoreWeave under a 12-year agreement that could bring in around $10 billion in revenue, the report adds, citing sources familiar with the deal. Core Scientific\u2019s move follows a string of large deals. Recent offerings tied to Google-backed data centers and CoreWeave raised a combined $6.7 billion. Another firm, Edged Compute, is marketing $1.3 billion in bonds to fund facilities leased to CoreWeave and an Alibaba unit. Core Scientific said it will use proceeds to repay existing debt and fund reserves. It also plans to support construction across several states if costs exceed available funds, signaling how capital-intensive the AI buildout has become. The company still holds \u201cunder 1,000 bitcoin,\u201d according to CFO Jim Nygaard. Big AI pivot Core Scientific was founded in 2017 and grew into one of North America's largest bitcoin miners before filing for Chapter 11 in December 2022, squeezed by high power costs and a weak bitcoin price. It emerged from reorganization in January 2024 and was relisted on Nasdaq under the ticker CORZ. The pivot from bitcoin mining to AI hosting is all about the margins. The April 2024 halving cut block rewards from 6.25 BTC to 3.125, and by late 2025, the average cash cost to mine one bitcoin rose while the price of BTC itself had been on a downturn, from"

    "published": "2026-04-23T06:00:54Z",
    "content": "Meta commits $21B to CoreWeave, signaling neoclouds as core AI infrastructure layer | Edge Infrastructure Review AI Infrastructure Cloud Platforms Connectivity Data Centers Edge Computing rss linkedin facebook search search --> About News Companies Markets Directory Guides Resources Events Subscribe Search for: Search X Share X/Tweet Link Comment Meta commits $21B to CoreWeave, signaling neoclouds as core AI infrastructure layer Apr 23, 2026 | Stephen Mayhew Categories Digital Infrastructure News | Technology & Architecture Neocloud provider, CoreWeave and Meta announced a $21 billion agreement to expand AI infrastructure, providing AI cloud capacity through December 2032. Meta is using its AI development and deployment alongside CoreWeave\u2019s AI cloud platform in partnership. The infrastructure will be distributed across multiple locations, including those located in deployments of NVIDIA Vera Rubin, to provide performance, resiliency and scalability. \u201cThis is another example that leading companies are choosing CoreWeave\u2019s AI cloud to run their most demanding workloads,\u201d says Michael Intrator, Co-founder, CEO, Chairman of CoreWeave. The deal underscores the increasing need for high-performance infrastructure in order to support demanding AI workloads at any scale. It also validates neoclouds as a critical layer in AI infrastructure. It highlights hyperscalers increasingly relying on external GPU-centric platforms to scale faster, manage supply constraints, and support distributed AI workloads, reinforcing the shift toward high-density, performance-optimized AI factory architectures. CoreWeave is an AI cloud powerhouse provider used by the largest AI labs, startups and enterprises.The company continued to grow its services and partnerships centered on AI, eventually going public on the Nasdaq in March 2025. Informa TechTarget | Channel Dive Cybersecurity Dive InformationWeek SearchCIO SearchEnterpriseAI Explore our brands An Informa TechTarget Publication Deep Dive Opinion Library Events Press Releases Topics Sign up Search Sign up Search IT Strategy Cloud Security Big Data AI Software Leadership An article from Dive Brief CoreWeave, Google Cloud link up for AI training, inference The AI cloud provider is among a growing list of vendors attempting to make it easier for clouds to work together. Published April 22, 2026 Makenzie Holland Senior News Writer Share Copy link Email LinkedIn X/Twitter Facebook Print License Add us on Google The CoreWeave name and logo displayed on billboards in Times Square on March 28, 2025, in NYC. CoreWeave is collaborating with Google Cloud to ease cross-cloud interconnectivity. Michael M. Santiago / Staff via Getty Images Listen to the article 3 min This audio is auto-generated. Please let us know if you have feedback . Dive Brief: CoreWeave is launching a trio of new capabilities aimed at simplifying cross-cloud AI training and inference, according to a Wednesday announcement. The new features include CoreWeave Interconnect, which directly links the AI cloud provider to Google Cloud and will be available soon in private preview for select regions. CoreWeave Interconnect uses Google Cloud\u2019s Partner Cross-Cloud Interconnect capability, which allows developers to run training and inference workloads where needed. The interconnectivity makes it easier for developers to build distributed AI-powered services, said Rob Enns, VP and general manager of cloud networking at Google Cloud, in the announcement. CoreWeave also launched SUNK Anywhere and LOTA Cross-Cloud. SUNK Anywhere supports large-scale, long-running AI training projects, allowing developers to add capacity acros s environments including Google Cloud, AWS, Microsoft Azure and on-premises. Meanwhile, LOTA Cross-Cloud gives AI builders the ability to store data in one place while running workloads across cloud environments. Dive Insight: CoreWeave\u2019s addition of cross-cloud capabilities supporting AI workloads comes as hyperscalers push to harness open cloud environments for enterprises deploying the technology at scale. In December, AWS and Google Cloud launched a joint multicloud collaboration creating an open specification for network interoperability and enabling customers to shift from manually connecting clouds to deploying cloud-native experiences. Microsoft Azure is set to join the collaboration later in 2026. \u201cWe are excited about this collaboration which enables our customers to move their data and applications between clouds with simplified global connectivity and enhanced operational effectiveness,\u201d Enns said in a press release at the time. While it spurred connectivity across multicloud environments, the collaboration also put pressure on other cloud providers to adopt Google Cloud and AWS\u2019 interoperability standard, Forrester Principal Analyst Lee Sustar told CIO Dive when the partnership"
  },

    "content": "News 12 | New Jersey | Ai Data Center Under Construction In Kenilworth Draws Mixed Reactions From Residents News 12 Toll of Commuting News12 New York Where to Watch Download the App LIVE Local Crime Weather beWell The East End Crime Files FIFA World Cup 'We're going to have major issues.' Kenilworth residents push back against AI data center under construction CoreWeave, a company that specializes in AI infrastructure, purchased a large portion of property on the campus in 2025 to build the center. Christine Queally \u2022 Apr 19, 2026, 10:20 PM \u2022 Updated Share: Top Stories 01:34 Zadies Bake Shop reopens 2 years after devastating fire 02:16 Sunny and warm weather returns briefly before a Saturday soaking Small earthquake detected near Bedminster State police seek information in 1970s Salem County cold case 01:51 About 30 Hoboken buildings without water as crews work on emergency repairs 00:18 Man charged in hit-and-run that killed 83-year-old Union City woman 00:26 Man accused of using tow truck to steal and crush 7 cars in Trenton 00:48 Road Trip Close To Home: Step into the world of Sesame Street at the American Dream Mall A $1.8 billion artificial intelligence data center under construction in Kenilworth is facing pushback from residents. The project is located at the Northeast Science and Technology Center (NEST) campus. The old Merck & Co. building will house the data center. CoreWeave, a company that specializes in AI infrastructure, purchased a large portion of property on the campus in 2025 to build the center. Despite the Kenilworth Borough Council's approval of the project, it has largely flown under the radar until recently. \"Social media, that's the only way you'll hear about it here even though we're only 200 yards from the location,\" said Union County resident Brent Krueger. Some residents told News 12 they're concerned about having a data center built near their homes. They mentioned concerns about water quality, flood risk and a potential increase in their electric bill. \"We're going to have major issues with the water in the future, with so many data centers being built. It's just not possible. We cannot sustain it. They're destroying the earth,\" said Union County resident Rosa Joseph. Neighbors even circulated a petition to stop the construction, with over 1,500 signatures as of Sunday night. \"Hopefully, this can happen. We still have time,\" Joseph said. Kenilworth Mayor Linda Karlovitch, however, said residents shouldn't see much of a difference, if any at all, once the center is operational. \"CoreWeave will be paying sales tax on the power, funding all required transmission upgrades. Those costs will not be passed on to residential rate payers,\" she said. \"I want people to know they're using a closed loop cooling system and it avoids continuous use of local water.\" Karlovitch told News 12 the borough is expecting significant economic payoff from CoreWeave's investment, including over 140 jobs. \"When Merck left, it left a real gap in jobs, in tax revenue, and CoreWeave is filling that gap and"

What this means for investors CoreWeave rose sharply on Tuesday after Nvidia committed $2 billion to the AI infrastructure provider, a move that investors read as a strong endorsement of CoreWeave\u2019s position in the fast-growing market for GPU-powered cloud services. The investment, priced at $87.20 a share, deepens ties between the chipmaker and CoreWeave\u2019s data center platform. Reports that Nvidia\u2019s stake could increase to roughly 11% and help fund plans for more than 5 gigawatts of AI data center capacity by 2030 added to the rally. The stock also benefited from a Deutsche Bank upgrade, which brought added institutional attention to the story. Core Scientific (NASDAQ: CORZ) grabbed headlines this week with its proposed $3.3 billion senior secured notes priced at 7.75%, the latest in a wave of high-yield debt offerings tied to the AI infrastructure boom. While the financing itself has been widely covered, a lesser-noticed supplemental filing tied to the deal offers something more revealing: a detailed look at the actual infrastructure assets being pledged to back the debt \u2014 and, more importantly, the cash flow profile expected to service it. At the center of the deal is Core Scientific\u2019s long-term hosting relationship with CoreWeave. The document shows that the two parties have already funded roughly 80% of the estimated $5.5 billion in total capex for the projects, underscoring how far along the buildout is. It also lays out how Core Scientific is packaging the CoreWeave-related data center assets into a financing vehicle with the underlying sites \u2014 and their future revenues \u2014 forming the backbone of the credit story. In doing so, it provides one of the clearest disclosures yet into how a Bitcoin miner\u2019s AI pivot is being translated into a project-financed, debt-backed business. A Site-by-Site Look at the Buildout Based on the document, Core Scientific is developing six data center facilities across five campuses, having secured roughly 900 MW of"

Search Blog Markets Non Sorted Data Processing Servers Apr 19, 2026 AI Infrastructure Boom: Neocloud Providers CoreWeave and Nebius Report Surging Revenue According to a recent report from Yahoo Finance, companies providing artificial intelligence infrastructure are currently experiencing significant growth and profitability. This sector is highlighted as a high-growth area for investment. Neocloud Providers Showcase Rapid Expansion Two notable companies in this space, CoreWeave and Nebius , are classified as neocloud providers, meaning they specialize in delivering AI-specific computing power. While both secure major contracts from large technology firms, their service models differ. CoreWeave concentrates primarily on providing graphics processing units, whereas Nebius offers a more comprehensive, full-stack solution for AI development. Financial Performance and Projections Nebius reported a substantial year-over-year revenue increase in the fourth quarter, reaching a figure of $228 million. The company anticipates its annual revenue run rate will grow significantly by the end of 2026. CoreWeave also demonstrated strong growth, with its fourth-quarter revenue rising year-over-year to $1.6 billion. CoreWeave holds a considerable backlog and expects to recognize a specific revenue total over the next two years. Another company mentioned alongside these firms is Applied Digital , which is also noted for its high growth rate within the AI infrastructure industry.     "content": "CoreWeave Takes As Much Financial Engineering As It Does Datacenter Design Jump to main content Search More topics Financial results IT spending Infrastructure Anthropic AMD Nvidia Google Microsoft Meta Platforms AWS OpenAI Database All the latest news, from all sections Search Compute Store Connect Control Code HPC AI Hyperscale Cloud Edge Enterprise NEXTPLATFORM AD CLOUD CoreWeave Takes As Much Financial Engineering As It Does Datacenter Design Timothy Prickett Morgan Timothy Prickett Morgan Co-Editor, Co-Founder, The Next Platform Published thu 9 Apr 2026 // 21:57 UTC Meta Platforms has aspirations to be one of the big AI model builders and to push the state of the art to \u201csuperintelligence,\u201d under the impression that it can sell access to personal AI agents to its more than 1 billion users worldwide. It is a reasonable business model \u2013 and a lot more sensible than the metaverse that was the obsession of company founder Mark Zuckerberg a few years and a few tens of billions of dollars ago. While the social network and AI model maker has a fondness for its own server, storage, switch, and datacenter designs, even after the launch of the Open Compute Project and its open strategy firmly charted, the company has not been shy about renting datacenter capacity when and where it needed to. And in the GenAI boom, Meta Platforms has not shied away from renting compute capacity from others. NEXTPLATFORM AD Back in September 2025, Meta Platforms inked a supplier deal with king of the neoclouds, CoreWeave, that saw the former buy $14.2 billion in AI processing capacity through December 2031 . This was a big deal for CoreWeave, which had just gone public and which needed to show that it has customers other than Microsoft renting its GPU hours. With the deal announced today in an 8-K filing with the US Securities and Exchange Commission , CoreWeave said that Meta Platforms has signed up for an additional $21 billion in AI processing capacity running out through December 2032. No one knows the ramp of that capacity usage, and it is no doubt nailed down pretty hard because the one thing CoreWeave can\u2019t have is a GPU burning capital and electricity and a customer not paying for the privilege of using it. It is not safe to assume that all $21 billion of spending happens in 2032, the incremental year, but for all we know that is exactly what will happen. This deal will bring CoreWeave\u2019s revenue backlog up to $87.8 billion, and Meta Platforms will represent 40.1 percent of that backlog. Back in March 2025 , OpenAI inked a five year deal with CoreWeave for $11.9 billion in capacity, and another $10.5 billion in capacity was added last year, bringing the total to $22.4 billion in capacity. Assuming that none of that OpenAI capacity has been activated as yet, that would represent 25.5 percent of that extended backlog. So these two customers \u2013 Meta Platforms and OpenAI \u2013 represent nearly two-thirds of the guaranteed revenue on"

Together with $1.75 billion of previously issued notes, CoreWeave now has $2.75 billion aggregate principal amount of these senior notes outstanding. The new notes were issued at 102.000% of principal , bear cash interest of 9.750% semi-annually , mature on October 1, 2031 , and are guaranteed on a senior unsecured basis by certain wholly owned subsidiaries. CoreWeave plans to use the proceeds for general corporate purposes, including repayment of outstanding debt and offering-related fees. Positive None. Previous Next --> Overview Financials News SEC Filings Barclays (DJP) offers buffered autocallable notes linked to CoreWeave (CRWV) Filing Impact (Neutral) Filing Sentiment (Neutral) Form Type 424B2 Rhea-AI Filing Summary English Italian Spanish German Korean French Arabic Barclays Bank PLC is offering Buffered Autocallable Contingent Coupon Notes due February 1, 2028 , linked to the Class A common stock of CoreWeave, Inc. The notes pay a contingent coupon of 6.0625% ( $60.625 per $1,000) on specified Observation Dates and feature an automatic call mechanism and a 40.00% buffer before downside leverage applies. If the Final Value is below the Buffer Value ( $69.10 ), the payoff at maturity applies a 1.666667 downside leverage, meaning investors can lose up to 100% of principal; the notes are unsecured obligations of Barclays and are subject to U.K. bail-in powers. Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 19, 2026 Five AI Stocks Under $200 for Investment in 2026 A recent article from Yahoo Finance notes that while fractional shares are increasingly available, some investors still face barriers to purchasing high-priced stocks, making reasonably priced full shares a component of certain investment approaches. The source identifies five companies considered worthy purchases for investors limited to a $200 per share budget, ranging from established entities to emerging businesses. Nvidia Nvidia stock trades near $201 per share. The company produces GPUs, which have become a primary computing unit since a competitive surge in artificial intelligence began in 2023. Wall Street analysts forecast revenue growth of 79% for the first quarter and 85% for the second quarter. Significant planned spending on AI infrastructure by large-scale operators is viewed as a positive indicator for the company's future. Nebius Nebius stock has nearly doubled in value this year. The company provides AI-focused computing hardware to clients, a focus driving notable growth. Analysts project revenue growth of 522% for this year and 195% for the following year, which represents the fastest growth rate among the listed stocks. These projections suggest potential for further stock price appreciation. CoreWeave CoreWeave operates in a comparable sector to Nebius, focusing on GPU-based cloud computing infrastructure. While its growth rate is lower, its overall scale is larger. At the end of 2027, Wall Street expects $23.3 billion in revenue for CoreWeave versus Nebius's $9.7 billion. Projected growth rates of 142% for 2026 and 87% for 2027 indicate strong analyst confidence in the company's products. The source concludes that both firms appear to be solid investment choices currently. 1. Top 3 stocks by turnover in the sector: Microsoft Corp (MSFT) up 1.68%; Palantir Technologies Inc (PLTR) up 4.10%; Alphabet Inc Class A (GOOGL) up 1.70%. What is driving CoreWeave Inc (CRWV)\u2019s stock price up today? CoreWeave (CRWV) experienced an notable upward price movement today, driven by a convergence of positive factors stemming from its strong position in the artificial intelligence (AI) cloud infrastructure sector and recent corporate developments. The company's stock has been benefiting from increasing demand for AI-optimized computing, with its market share in AI-optimized infrastructure projected to expand significantly by 2028. A key driver appears to be the successful closing of a private offering of $1 billion aggregate principal amount of 9.750% Senior Notes due 2031, which concluded on April 21, 2026. This financing is intended for general corporate purposes, including repayment of outstanding indebtedness, and provides capital to support CoreWeave's aggressive expansion plans in the high-growth AI space. Furthermore, the company has recently secured substantial new contracts that significantly bolster its revenue backlog. These include a six-year agreement with Meta Platforms announced on April 9, 2026, for AI cloud capacity, as well as a multi-year deal with Anthropic disclosed on April 10, 2026, to support its AI model development. Additionally, Jane Street signed a $6 billion AI cloud agreement around April 15, 2026. These major deals contribute to CoreWeave's robust backlog, which reached $66.8 billion by the fourth quarter of 2025 and is anticipated to approach $100 billion by the current quarter, providing strong visibility into future revenue streams. Analyst sentiment remains predominantly positive, with multiple firms issuing or maintaining \"Buy\" or \"Overweight\" ratings and increasing price targets throughout April 2026. For instance, Cantor Fitzgerald raised its price target to $156 on April 16, 2026, and Wolfe Research initiated coverage with a \"Buy\" rating and a $150 price target on the same day. The average analyst price target suggests further upside for the stock. Institutional activity also contributed to positive sentiment, with Mirae Asset Global Investments Co. Both stocks have pulled back sharply from their 2025 highs. Wall Street\u2019s rapidly expanding analyst coverage of both names suggests institutional conviction is building, though Nebius commands a higher valuation multiple and a stronger margin outlook. Now Live: Discover how much upside your favorite stocks could have using TIKR\u2019s new Valuation Model (It\u2019s free) >>> Two artificial intelligence infrastructure plays are grabbing investor attention right now. Both are building the data centers that power the AI boom. Both are early-stage, burning cash, and priced on future potential rather than today\u2019s profits. But Wall Street doesn\u2019t treat them equally \u2014 and the gap is wider than you might think. Nebius Group ( NBIS ) and Iren Limited ( IREN ) are chasing the same tailwind: surging demand for GPU compute. The question is which one has stronger analyst conviction. The numbers tell a clear story. Estimate a company\u2019s fair value instantly (Free with TIKR) >>> Why traditional earnings metrics don\u2019t work here Before comparing the two, it helps to understand what each company does. Nebius is the Amsterdam-listed rebuild of Yandex\u2019s core AI and cloud infrastructure business. After geopolitical events forced its founding team out of Russia in 2022, chief executive Arkady Volozh and hundreds of engineers relocated to Europe and restarted from scratch. Today, Nebius operates a full-stack AI cloud \u2014 think GPU clusters, managed storage, and developer tools \u2014 targeting AI start-ups and enterprises that need compute but can\u2019t afford to build their own. At a Morgan Stanley conference in March 2026, Volozh described the ambition plainly: the company is targeting nearly one gigawatt of active power this year and has a pipeline reserved for up to three gigawatts. \u201cDemand far exceeds our supply,\u201d Nebius Chief Revenue Officer Marc Boroditsky told the audience, according to the conference transcript. Iren is an Australian-founded company that spent seven years as a Bitcoin miner before pivoting hard into AI cloud hosting. It now operates 810 megawatts of existing data centers \u2014 predominantly air-cooled, which is increasingly in demand \u2014 and has secured a $9.7 billion AI contract with Microsoft. Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 20, 2026 Iren Expands AI Data Center Capacity to 4.5 Gigawatts According to a report from Yahoo Finance , Iren Limited has significantly expanded its portfolio of power-connected land for data centers. The company, noted for its vertically integrated model of owning both land and facilities, recently secured an additional 1.6 gigawatts of grid-connected property in Oklahoma . This acquisition complements its existing 160 megawatts of capacity in Canada and a 2.75 gigawatt footprint across two sites in Texas, bringing its total potential capacity to 4.5 gigawatts. The company's strategic shift from Bitcoin mining to providing computing for artificial intelligence applications has coincided with a substantial increase in its stock price over the preceding twelve months. Despite this rise, the stock remains below its historical peak. A key factor in its recent performance is a major agreement with Microsoft , which is projected to generate nearly $2 billion in annualized revenue from 200 megawatts of capacity at high project-level margins. Based on the financial terms of that agreement, analysts project that fully utilizing the entire 4.5 gigawatt portfolio could result in annual revenue exceeding $40 billion. Should the margin structure from the Microsoft deal hold, the associated adjusted EBITDA could approach $37 billion, before accounting for corporate expenses. Currently, the company has projected annual recurring revenue for the year from just over ten percent of its total capacity. 1. Latest news Video Bitcoin DeFi NFT Ethereum Altcoins Blockchain Mining Finance Metaverse Legal Security Analytics Exchange Other GameFi ICO Latest news Video Bitcoin DeFi NFT Ethereum Altcoins Blockchain Mining Finance Metaverse Legal Security Analytics Exchange Other GameFi ICO Back to the list IREN rides Bitcoin mining-era power infrastructure to lead AI data center race cryptopolitan.com 19 h IREN may win the data center game, based on its available energy contracts from the era of Bitcoin mining. The company has a head start in building data centers, where access to reliable energy is turning into a key bottleneck. Iren Limited (Nasdaq:IREN) recently drew attention to $BTC mining stocks and may signal a shift in general sentiment for mining companies. Traditionally, IREN has tracked the price of $BTC and the overall crypto sentiment. As of April 2026, IREN has entered a key pivot stage, when the stock may start to reflect the AI narrative, while depending less on tracking the crypto market. IREN traded at around $45.12, bouncing from the year\u2019s lows at $31.62. IREN recovered in April, drawing attention to its significant energy contract portfolio. | Source: Google Finance IREN is closely watched as the most active Bitcoin mining stock, which may lead to recoveries in other assets. Most of the leading Bitcoin mining stocks are in the green for the year to date, based on recent market performance data . IREN still attracts short open interest IREN has seen regular spikes in short open interest. As of April 22, short open interest is at 18.42% of the stock\u2019s free float. IREN short positions are still small compared to the short open interest for Mara Holdings (Nasdaq: MARA) at up to 30%, as well as CleanSpark (Nasdaq: CLSK) . Despite this, traders aim to grab the opportunity for shorting IREN, benefiting from daily swings. Bitcoin mining stocks are shorted in expectation of a diminishing role for miners. Yet miners may still retain earnings from their $BTC operations, sit on significant reserves, and retain some of their self-mining activity. IREN may avoid the main bottleneck for US-based AI data centers IREN is one of the largest holders of power access in the Bitcoin mining space. The company owns land and its own substations. The company has built a portfolio of locations using secure supply in Texas, with a 1.4 GW facility in Sweetwater and a total of 2.75 GW signed for all Texas campuses. The Sweetwater 1 facility is expected to launch by the end of April with 1.4 GW of energy. The company will keep adding access, with another center expected to access 750 MW. IREN is positioned with"

We also understand that projects like this raise important questions. We are committed to operating responsibly, respecting local priorities, and minimizing environmental impact while delivering reliable digital infrastructure. Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 15, 2026 AI Data Center Investments Face Delays, IREN Secures Key Advantages According to a recent report from Yahoo Finance , substantial capital is being directed toward the construction of artificial intelligence data centers. Amazon and Alphabet are among the firms making large-scale commitments for such facilities in Mississippi and Texas , respectively. Despite this influx of investment, a significant portion of data center projects across the United States have encountered delays or cancellations. These setbacks are attributed to supply chain constraints and dependencies on imported components. Such challenges increase the value of existing operational data centers and provide an advantage to companies that began their infrastructure development earlier. IREN Secures Energy and Chips for AI Operations IREN has positioned itself by securing a substantial amount of renewable energy capacity for its data centers, exceeding 4.5 gigawatts following an acquisition in Oklahoma. Energy access is a critical constraint, with grid interconnection processes often taking years. The company currently operates data centers with a capacity of 810 megawatts and expects to energize a major new site imminently. IREN has contracted annual recurring revenue of $2.3 billion, anchored by a multi-year agreement with Microsoft for 200 megawatts of capacity. IREN differentiates its business model by purchasing advanced chips directly for its data centers, which increases its value per megawatt compared to firms that require clients to supply their own hardware. The company recently expanded its fleet of graphics processing units with a large new order. 1.     "content": "Exclusive: TD Synnex Nabs Over 1,000 Nvidia GPUs For Channel In Nebius Cloud Deal Exclusive: TD Synnex Nabs Over 1,000 Nvidia GPUs For Channel In Nebius Cloud Deal TD Synnex officials Francisco Criado and Jack Donato tell CRN that the Nebius partnership will help channel partners gain access to high-end GPUs in the cloud, which have become a scarce resource due to high demand from AI-native companies. TD Synnex has reserved over 1,000 high-end Nvidia GPUs in the cloud to help solution providers overcome supply constraints and get their customers moving on AI projects. The U.S. distribution giant, which won Nvidia\u2019s Distribution Partner of the Year award for the Americas last month , made the cloud commitment for AI computing clusters based on Nvidia\u2019s B300 GPU with neocloud provider Nebius. TD Synnex announced the deal on Tuesday at its High-Growth Conference in Puerto Rico. [Related: Nvidia Channel Chief: Neoclouds Represent \u2018Next Phase Of Opportunity\u2019 For Partners ] The Fremont, Calif.-based company is accepting purchase orders for B300-based instances through Nebius now, with availability expected to begin in July. The agreement lasts for a year, and it gives TD Synnex the option for renewal. Nvidia Americas channel chief Craig Weinstein told CRN last month that the company sees the partnership as an important way to reach enterprise customers. \u201cIt means that we have an [Nvidia Cloud Partner] that\u2019s going to offer cloud capability to the ecosystem to ensure [that] enterprises can iterate and solution providers have a platform to leverage,\u201d he said. TD Synnex-Nebius Deal Seen As Way To Combat Supply Scarcity In an exclusive interview with CRN, TD Synnex officials Francisco Criado and Jack Donato said that the Nebius partnership will help channel partners gain access to what has become a scarce resource due to high demand from AI-native companies. \u201cThere\u2019s literally no capacity available right now,\u201d said Criado, senior vice president of cloud, security and AI at TD Synnex. TD Synnex expects the Nebius deal to benefit many of its strategic partners who otherwise \u201cwouldn\u2019t get the prioritization that they would need to gain access to the GPUs,\u201d according to Donato, senior director AI and neocloud vendor solutions at TD Synnex. \u201cOur ability to give them that prioritization up there with the general hyperscalers and neocloud providers just gives them access, in general,\u201d he said. \u201cCould they get the same capacity direct? Maybe, but there\u2019s no availability.\u201d TD Synnex: Deal Represents First Of Its Kind In Disty World According to TD Synnex, the Nebius engagement \u201cmarks the first time a global IT distributor has reserved a dedicated Nvidia AI factory-grade cluster from an AI-native cloud provider.\u201d \u201cIt\u2019s not your typical distribution go-to-market where you\u2019re buying and reselling something,\u201d Criado said. \u201cWe\u2019re actually committing and we\u2019ve purchased this reserved instance within the Nebius data center ... and we will now work with our partners and their customers to [drive consumption].\u201d Based on Nvidia\u2019s Blackwell Ultra architecture, the B300 debuted last year as the company\u2019s flagship GPU for x86-based servers that"

Calcalist previously reported that the Israeli startup had held advanced discussions with Nvidia at a reported valuation of $2 billion to $3 billion, above its last known valuation of $1.4 billion from 2023. Those talks reached senior levels but ultimately did not materialize into a transaction. 1 View gallery AI21 Labs founders. ( Photo: Orel Cohen ) In January, co-founder and chairman Amnon Shashua confirmed that AI21 was engaged in discussions with Nvidia as well as other potential buyers, indicating that the company was formally exploring strategic options. Founded in 2017 by Shashua, Professor Yoav Shoham and Ori Goshen, AI21 was among the early companies developing large-scale language models. However, it has struggled in recent years to keep pace with leading AI developers such as OpenAI and Anthropic. The company has since narrowed its focus to enterprise applications, emphasizing accuracy and reliability. It discontinued development of its consumer product, Wordtune, and now centers on offerings such as its Maestro platform and a recently introduced reasoning model. Annual revenue is estimated at around $50 million. Related articles: \u201cThere are talks with Nvidia and others\u201d: Shashua confirms AI21 acquisition discussions AI21 never closed its reported $300 million round as Nvidia weighs an acquisition Nvidia in advanced talks to acquire AI21 in $2-3 billion deal focused on talent AI21 employs roughly 200 people, down from about 260 in 2023, after scaling back expansion plans. Much of the interest in the company has been attributed to its workforce, which includes highly trained AI researchers. The earlier Nvidia talks were widely viewed as driven primarily by this talent base, rather than by AI21\u2019s commercial scale.     "content": "Nebius Group Gains Channel Reach As TD SYNNEX Debuts GPU Clusters - Simply Wall St News Home Portfolios Watchlist Community Discover Screener Sign Up or Login Home Portfolios Watchlist Community Discover Screener Advertisement United States / Software / NasdaqGS:NBIS Nebius Group Gains Channel Reach As TD SYNNEX Debuts GPU Clusters April 18, 2026 Simply Wall St Reviewed by Bailey Pemberton TD SYNNEX launched a global reseller GPU cluster offering on Nebius AI Cloud, reserved for partner use. The partnership introduces production-grade NVIDIA AI factory-grade GPU clusters to TD SYNNEX's worldwide channel. Nebius Group, listed as NasdaqGS:NBIS, becomes the first AI native cloud provider to supply this type of dedicated, globally distributed GPU capacity through a major IT distributor. For Nebius Group, this move puts its AI focused cloud infrastructure directly into the hands of TD SYNNEX's partner network, which serves a broad base of enterprise customers. Instead of only relying on large hyperscaler contracts, Nebius is now positioned as an AI infrastructure option that channel partners can package into their own solutions for clients that want predictable, production ready compute. For investors, this collaboration illustrates how Nebius Group is being incorporated into the broader enterprise technology stack rather than being limited to the largest technology companies. The scale and structure of this reseller GPU cluster help show how AI workloads are being sourced and standardized across the channel, and how Nebius is seeking to participate in that spending over time.     "content": "Digest: DSP Bedrock Platform Pilots Index Exchange's Index Cloud; Meta Sued Over Scam Ads; Radiocentre Urges MPs to Reject BBC Ads - ExchangeWire.com \u00d7 Home Features Events London Singapore Dubai Tokyo Cannes Madrid Event videos Event photos Awards Podcast MadTech Podcast Video TraderTalk TV A Coffee With\u2026 Webcasts Video Interviews VoxPops Event Videos Sketches Columnists Charlotte McEleny Shirley Marschall Peggy Anne Salz Chloe Singleton Jessica Treasure Reports ExchangeWire Research Deep Dive Ebooks Industry Review Insights & Attitudes Indexes PressBox News German French Spanish Italian Profiles Hot Topics MENA Predictions AI Retail Media CTV Privacy Attention Curation About us Contact Us Our websites ExchangeWire.com \u2713 ExchangeWire.jp Home Features Events London Singapore Dubai Tokyo Cannes Madrid Event videos Event photos Awards Podcast MadTech Podcast Video TraderTalk TV A Coffee With\u2026 Webcasts Video Interviews VoxPops Event Videos Sketches Columnists Charlotte McEleny Shirley Marschall Peggy Anne Salz Chloe Singleton Jessica Treasure Reports ExchangeWire Research Deep Dive Ebooks Industry Review Insights & Attitudes Indexes PressBox News German French Spanish Italian Profiles Hot Topics MENA Predictions AI Retail Media CTV Privacy Attention Curation Digest: DSP Bedrock Platform Pilots Index Exchange's Index Cloud; Meta Sued Over Scam Ads; Radiocentre Urges MPs to Reject BBC Ads by Sheila Obeng-King on 23rd Apr 2026 in News In today\u2019s Digest, we discuss Bedrock Platform piloting Index Exchange\u2019s new Index Cloud, the Consumer Federation of America suing Meta Platforms over alleged scam advertising practices, and Radiocentre urging MPs to reject advertising on BBC platforms. DSP Bedrock Platform Pilots Index Exchange's Index Cloud Index Exchange has launched Index Cloud, a new platform designed to enable models, data, applications, and bidders to operate directly at the point of transaction. The move builds on more than two decades of development for the open internet, now adapted to support an AI-native programmatic ecosystem. Index Exchange have teamed with Bedrock Platform to showcase its capabilities, launching the world\u2019s first containerised DSP using the Index Cloud infrastructure. The company is positioning its new neutral compute environment, Index Cloud, as a way to reduce costs and improve efficiency in programmatic auctions, while also fostering more competitive dynamics across the DSP market. Meta sued over scam ads The Consumer Federation of America (CFA) has filed a lawsuit against Meta, alleging the company\u2019s handling of scam content on its platforms violates Washington, DC consumer protection laws. The complaint centres on fraudulent advertising, which CFA claims Meta allowed to proliferate despite public commitments to combat scams. Examples cited include ads promoting fake government benefits, such as USD$1,400 (\u00a31,106) stimulus payments and free government-issued phones, which were reportedly identified through Meta\u2019s own ads library. CFA argues that Meta profited from these ads and failed to adequately prevent their spread, and is seeking damages and reforms to the company\u2019s ad review processes. Meta Platforms did not immediately respond to a request for comment.     "content": "Meta's latest AI move is raising questions among employees \u2014 TradingView News Search Products Community Markets Brokers More EN Get started News / GuruFocus / Meta's latest AI move is raising questions among employees Meta's latest AI move is raising questions among employees Less than 1 min read META GOOG Meta META is going all in on improving its AI, but the way it is doing it is starting to feel a bit uneasy, especially for people inside the company. From what is being reported, Meta is tracking how employees actually use their computers, things like typing, clicking, and moving through apps like Google GOOG , LinkedIn, GitHub, and Slack. The thinking behind it is pretty logical. If you want AI to handle real world tasks, it needs to learn from how people actually do those tasks, not just from clean, structured data. So in that sense, this is about teaching AI how work really happens. But once you move past that, it gets a bit more complicated. Some employees are not comfortable with how much is being captured, especially when it comes to sensitive information that might show up on screen. Meta has said there are safeguards and that the data is only being used to train models, but the concern is less about intent and more about how far this could go. Published April 22, 2026 By Andrew Hutchinson Content and Social Media Manager Share Copy link Email LinkedIn X/Twitter Facebook Print License Add us on Google Listen to the article 3 min This audio is auto-generated. Please let us know if you have feedback . Meta has broken ground on its latest data center project in the U.S. , which marks a further expansion of the company\u2019s artificial intelligence processing capacity, and another step in its $600 billion commitment to developing U.S. infrastructure projects over the next three years. The Tulsa, Oklahoma-based data center will be the company\u2019s 28th in the U.S., its 32nd globally , and the first in the state. It will provide support for Meta\u2019s expanding AI workloads, in order to power the next stage of its Superintelligence push . Indeed, these days, Meta is almost as much a data center infrastructure company as a social media business. Meta is pushing to build its data infrastructure as quickly as it can, in order to accelerate its AI development. The company is also looking to get as much done as possible while U.S. President Donald Trump is in office, in order to take advantage of Trump\u2019s support for the expansion of U.S. business interests. In the White House\u2019s AI action plan, which was published in July 2025 , the government outlined the need to support U.S. businesses in order to ensure the nation\u2019s \u201c global dominance in artificial intelligence. \u201d The plan also set out specific measures to remove \u201cred tape and onerous regulation.\u201d That has seemingly helped accelerate Meta\u2019s broader AI development push, though Meta also knows that this more open approach may not last into the next administration. As such, Meta\u2019s is looking to get as many data centers up and running as possible over the next three years. At the same time, the company has committed to ensuring water and electricity support for the regions in which it\u2019s building. Meta has also pledged support for local community projects in an effort to play a positive role in regional economies. Auto Meta Rolls Out AI Parental Monitoring for Teen Accounts Globally | The Tech Buzz the tech buzz Meta Rolls Out AI Parental Monitoring for Teen Accounts Globally Articles Newsletters Articles Newsletters AI / AI safety Meta Rolls Out AI Parental Monitoring for Teen Accounts Globally Parents can now see topics teens discuss with Meta AI across Instagram, Facebook by The Tech Buzz PUBLISHED: Thu, Apr 23, 2026, 11:47 AM UTC | UPDATED: Thu, Apr 23, 2026, 2:27 PM UTC The Buzz \u25a0 Meta launches AI conversation insights for parents supervising teen accounts in US, UK, Australia, Canada, and Brazil, with global rollout coming soon \u25a0 New Insights tab shows topic categories teens discussed with Meta AI over past week, including School, Entertainment, Health and Wellbeing \u25a0 US teen enrollment in parental supervision has more than doubled since last year, according to Meta's announcement \u25a0 Meta establishes AI Wellbeing Expert Council with members from National Council for Suicide Prevention, University of Michigan, and USC Meta just gave parents a window into their teens' AI conversations. The company's rolling out new parental supervision tools that show what topics teens are asking Meta AI about - from homework help to health questions - starting in five countries and expanding globally within weeks. It's Meta's latest response to mounting pressure around AI safety for minors, following October's announcement that promised greater parental oversight of teen AI interactions. Meta is betting that transparency beats secrecy when it comes to teens and AI. The company just flipped the switch on parental monitoring tools that let parents peek at what their kids are asking Meta AI - without revealing the exact questions themselves. Starting today, parents using supervision on Facebook , Messenger, or Instagram will see a new Insights tab. Click through and you'll find broad topic categories their teen explored with Meta AI over the past seven days. Think School, Entertainment, Lifestyle, Travel, Writing, Health and Wellbeing - pretty much the gamut of teenage curiosity, according to Meta's official blog post . But Meta's walking a fine line"

Credit: Timon Schneider/SOPA Images/LightRocket via Getty Images Meta is about to ramp up surveillance of its employees, Reuters reports, but in a very 2026 twist, it's not meant to catch people slacking off. Reuters reports that Meta is installing tracking software that can capture mouse movements and keystrokes on U.S.-based employees' computers. While this sort of surveillance isn't unheard of in corporate America, the motivation here is slightly novel: Meta is reportedly going to use the data to train AI agents, per a company memo seen by Reuters. This will be done through a tool called Model Capability Initiative, or MCI. Spent) Apr 23, 2026 Meta Partners with Noon Energy for Long-Duration Storage to Power Data Centers Meta Platforms has agreed to reserve a significant amount of energy storage capacity from Noon Energy for its data center operations, according to a report from Power Technology . The initial phase of the collaboration involves a 25MW project expected to be completed by 2028, with the potential for a much larger supply arrangement to follow. The planned systems will utilize Noon Energy's technology, which is designed to provide continuous power over multiple days. This capability is intended to maintain consistent data center operations during periods when renewable energy generation is insufficient. The agreement supports the company's infrastructure goals and its efforts to combine renewable sources with a reliable energy supply. Noon Energy will manage development of the initial project. The company's focus is on ultra-long duration energy storage, which aims to mitigate challenges related to intermittent renewable generation. Meta's leadership stated that rapid deployment of reliable energy is necessary to bring data centers online faster, and that this agreement advances that goal with a technology that delivers grid resilience. Noon Energy's CEO noted that data centers are a strong application for its battery system and expressed anticipation for building production capacity and a supply chain in the coming years. This agreement follows another recent power purchase agreement for a solar plant in Texas , expanding the company's existing clean energy collaborations. See also>>> MONEY FOR META: How will the new data center be funded? The City of Tulsa will also receive $62.5 million in infrastructure funding from Meta, instead of taxes, as part of a 25-year tax-incentive district (TID) agreement. Meta will only pay 15% of its property tax bill during that time. However, some officials are not celebrating as elected and economic leaders did during the announcement. Previous coverage>>> META TO TULSA: Data center planned for east Tulsa supports Meta Count City Councilor Laura Bellis , who led the vote on a city data center moratorium through the end of the year, as not a fan. Bellis said she and her council colleagues repeatedly got the runaround from Meta officials throughout the process, and that the corporation has not agreed to any public meetings beyond local organizations. A Meta spokesperson told 2 News Oklahoma April 22 that with the AI data center , Meta will \u201cstrive to be good neighbors in the communities where we operate, and we make every effort to work with our local partners to address community needs.\u201d As we shared during our event yesterday, we are proud to call east Tulsa home. We strive to be good neighbors in the communities where we operate, and we make every effort to work with our local partners to address community needs. These scams include: \u201cPump and dump\u201d scams that trick investors into buying hyped stocks or cryptocurrencies, driving up prices so scammers can sell at a profit and leaving victims with losses; \u201cConfidence\u201d scams, in which fraudsters develop trusting relationships with their victims and convince them to \u201cinvest\u201d using fake investment platforms that drain the victims\u2019 money; and Fraudulent cryptocurrency schemes that use digital currencies to deceive people into giving away money or assets, often with the false promise of high returns or exclusive opportunities. These platforms make it very easy for bad actors to reach large audiences quickly and with little oversight, which is why they\u2019re fertile ground for investment scams. Preventing and stopping these fraudulent schemes in their tracks is an essential part of our work to make it easier for New Jerseyans to afford everyday life.\u201d \u201cSocial media investment scams are designed to get people hyped up about exciting opportunities to make big money with little to no risk. The goal is to get investors to jump in quickly without taking time to vet the legitimacy of the opportunity, the people promoting it, or the risks involved,\u201d said Jeremy E. Careers \u2018Venus in Fur\u2019 at Attic Salt is meta, intense, intimate theater \"Venus in Fur,\" directed by Jeff Catanese and starring Kate McGunagle and Alex McDonald Villarreal, is an intimate, psychological two-hander about sadomasochism, 19th century literature and the power dynamics between actors and their directors. By Pruett Norris April 22, 2026 6:31 pm Facebook X More LinkedIn Email SMS Contributed Jeff Catanese Kate McGunagle, left, and Alex McDonald Villarreal, right, as Vanda and Thomas in \"Venus in Fur.\" ASHEVILLE, N.C. ( 828newsNOW ) \u2014 \u201cVenus in Fur,\u201d directed by Jeff Catanese and starring Kate McGunagle and Alex McDonald Villarreal, is an intimate, psychological two-hander about sadomasochism, 19th century literature and the power dynamics between actors and their directors. The show features lighting design by Rachel Cort, costumes by McKinney Gough, intimacy and fight coordination by Gabby Bailey, sound design by Roger Bindy and set design by Falluja Rain. McGunagle stars as Vanda and Villarreal as Thomas. The Attic Salt Theatre Company play will run through this weekend, April 24-26. Performances will be held at 7:30 p.m. on Friday, 2:30 p.m. and 7:30 p.m. on Saturday and 2:30 p.m. on Sunday. The show lasts an hour and 55 minutes without intermission and contains adult themes and strong language. Find tickets here. \u201cVenus in Fur\u201d review For fans of Attic Salt, a lot about \u201cVenus in Fur\u201d will feel familiar. The show shares a similar construction to \u201cMiss Julie,\u201d staged at the black box theater last September: Small cast, big ideas and a lot to say about 19th century relationship dynamics. Read our review of \u201cMiss Julie\u201d here. To make matters more meta, that version of \u201cMiss Julie\u201d was a new adaptation by Attic Salt artistic director Jeff Catanese, director of \u201cVenus in Fur.\u201d In a fun, amusing reflection, \u201cVenus in Fur\u201d follows Thomas (Villarreal), a theater director and playwright casting his own adaptation of \u201cVenus in Furs,\u201d an 1870 erotic novella by Leopold von Sacher-Masoch. That said, \u201cVenus in Fur\u201d is entirely enjoyable on its own merits, no prior experience with adapting 19th-century literature required. Like all plays with a small cast, \u201cVenus in Fur\u201d lives and dies on the talent of its leads. Fortunately, Villarreal and McGunagle were both excellent. As Thomas, Villarreal played up his character with perfect repugnant pomposity. He was hilarious. In Vanda, meanwhile, McGunagle brought a brash, electric energy to the stage. The actors had great"

According to an internal memo obtained by Reuters, Meta is rolling out a new internal system designed to track how employees interact with their computers in real time. The initiative, known internally as the \u201cModel Capability Initiative,\u201d records mouse movements, keystrokes, clicks, and even screenshots across designated work applications. The directive is clear\u2014employees are expected to simply go about their daily tasks while the system quietly captures their behavior as training data for Meta\u2019s next generation of AI tools. Zuckerberg: Message to Meta Employees Raises Eyebrows \u2013 Your Work Is Now Training AI No Opt-Out, No Ambiguity What makes this policy particularly controversial is the lack of employee choice. Workers reportedly cannot opt out of the tracking system, transforming what might otherwise be framed as voluntary participation into a mandatory condition of employment. Meta has defended the move, stating that in order to build AI systems capable of performing real-world tasks, it needs authentic human behavior data\u2014how people click, navigate menus, and complete workflows on a computer. But critics argue the message behind the policy is unmistakable: employees are not just workers\u2014they are data sources. Building AI That Can Replace Human Tasks The initiative is part of a broader push by Meta to develop advanced AI \u201cagents\u201d capable of performing white-collar tasks autonomously. These systems are being trained to replicate the way humans interact with"

NVIDIA ( NASDAQ: NVDA ) recently launched what it described as the world\u2019s first open artificial intelligence (AI) model to deliver breakthrough performance for quantum computers, while Meta ( NASDAQ: META ) is collaborating with chip designer Broadcom to power its AI ambition. NVIDIA rolls out Ising \u2014 a breakthrough for AI-based quantum processor In a press release published by NVIDIA on April 14, the tech giant announced that NVIDIA Ising was made to \u201chelp researchers and enterprises build quantum processors capable of running useful applications.\u201d Named after a significant mathematical model that simplifies the analysis of complex physical systems, the NVIDIA Ising aims to provide high-performance AI tools for quantum error correction and calibration. The Ising model would enable researchers to tackle much larger and more complex problems with quantum computers by delivering up to 2.5x faster performance and 3x better accuracy during the decoding process, NVIDIA\u2019s press release said. It includes state-of-the-art customizable models, tools, as well as data such as Ising Calibration and Ising Decoding, to improve the use of quantum processors. With the Ising Calibration, users and AI agents can quickly interpret and react to measurements from quantum processors, enabling automating continuous calibration and, as a result, reducing the time needed from days to hours. While the Ising Decoding optimized speed and accuracy by performing real-time decoding for quantum error correction. It is up to 3x more accurate than pyMatching, the current open source industry standard. \u201cAI is essential to making quantum computing practical,\u201d Jensen Huang, founder and CEO of NVIDIA, expressed. \u201cWith Ising, AI becomes the control plane \u2014 the operating system of quantum machines \u2014 transforming fragile qubits to scalable and reliable quantum-GPU systems.\u201d NVIDIA reported that the quantum computing market is projected to exceed $11 billion by 2030, according to the analyst firm Resonance. This growth is driven by continued advancements in overcoming key engineering challenges such as quantum error correction and scalability. NVIDIA Ising joins the tech company\u2019s open model portfolio, such as NVIDIA Nemotron, NVIDIA Cosmos, NVIDIA Alpamayo, NVIDIA Isaac, and NVIDIA BioNeMo. The new open AI model is already being used by Academia Sinica, Fermi National Accelerator Laboratory, Harvard John A. Paulson School of Engineering and Applied Sciences, Infleqtion, IQM Quantum Computers,"

The move reflects growing pressure across the AI industry to find novel training data sources, as companies increasingly turn to unconventional repositories \u2014 including, separately, archived corporate communications from defunct startups. The GPU-native AI infrastructure provider will deploy NVIDIA Quantum-X800 infiniBand photonics co-packaged optics in an AI factory with 10,000+ NVIDIA Blackwell Ultra GPUs. Lambda\u2019s bare metal instances made its way out of the lab and into the core cloud offering, giving users direct access to hardware while avoiding virtualization overhead for distributed AI training workloads. Designed for launching thousands of parallel AI environments, the NVIDIA Vera CPU platform enables maximally high memory bandwidth which optimizes reinforcement learning and agentic AI workloads. The NVIDIA STX is a modular architecture for AI storage that augments inference, analytics, and training with next-gen hardware optimized KV-cache management. Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) networking enables faster, cost-efficient AI infrastructure suitable for large-scale AI factories, alleviating major efficiency bottlenecks found in current approaches. \u201cThe race to build AI factories isn\u2019t won on GPU counts alone,\u201d says Dave Salvator, director of accelerated computing at NVIDIA. \u201cNetwork architecture is what determines whether those systems can perform at scale. Getting this right is what allows AI infrastructure to power services used by hundreds of millions of people around the world.\u201c\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b Lambda oversees one of the largest deployments of NVIDIA Quantum-X800 CPO switches, highlighting how critical network architecture is when scaling AI systems. These announcements further bolster Lambda\u2019s AI infrastructure platform, which empowers frontier labs, enterprises, and hyperscalers with proven and energy-efficient workhorses built for reliability at scale. Lambda continues its mission to make AI compute ubiquitous, leveraging a decade-long collaboration with NVIDIA to advance its Superintelligence Cloud platform. These edge zones deliver AI compute capabilities in location-specific areas based on geography, and the use cases, not supported by traditional hyperscale providers. \u201cCrusoe Edge Zones powered by Crusoe Spark represent the continued expansion of our vertically integrated \u2018 AI Factory \u2019 vision,\u201d says Cully Cavness, co-founder, president, and chief strategy officer of Crusoe. \u201cBy optimizing these modular AI factories to run both the Crusoe Cloud platform and our Managed Inference product, we are delivering a high-performance, distributed solution that provides the speed, sovereignty, and quality that the next generation of AI requires.\u201d Crusoe can quickly deploy new cloud zones, within three months in some instances, expanding cost-saving AI capacity due to its vertically integrated approach. The main use cases are low-latency inference, dedicated enterprise clusters in multiple locations and sovereign AI deployments to serve regulated industries and governments. Crusoe envisions large-scale campuses for model training overhead and modular distributed compute for high-performance edge delivery. The company focuses on driving energy efficient, scalable AI infrastructure in its mission to accelerate energy and intelligence abundance. Organizations can reach out to Crusoe for deployment opportunities if they have geographic expansion, low-latency, or sovereign infrastructure requirements. By Craig Stephens Updated: March 25, 2026 This page contains links to our partners. We may be compensated when a link is clicked. Read the disclosures to learn more. News Archive Conclusion Notable Crusoe News 03/25/2026: Crusoe raising round at up to $40B valuation 10/23/2025: Crusoe raising $1.38 billion in latest round 09/30/2025: Crusoe announces Abilene data center is live 08/21/2025: Crusoe in Talks for $10 Billion Valuation Older news\u2026 About Crusoe Crusoe is a Denver-based AI computing company that builds cloud and AI infrastructure powered by cleaner energy sources, including wasted, stranded, and renewable energy. Founded in 2018 by Chase Lochmiller and Cully Cavness, Crusoe initially captured excess natural gas from oil fields and converted it into power for data centers, thereby reducing both costs and carbon emissions. Its primary business today focuses on sustainable cloud computing, offering GPU-based (e.g., Nvidia chips) infrastructure for AI workloads. The Crusoe Cloud platform is designed to provide high-performance computing while minimizing environmental impact. The name \u201cCrusoe\u201d reflects the idea of resourcefulness \u2014 using \u201cstranded\u201d energy, like Robinson Crusoe, making use of what\u2019s available in a remote environment. Positioned as a greener alternative to traditional data centers, Crusoe competes with hyperscalers and \u201cneo cloud\u201d providers like CoreWeave, Nebius, and Lambda, but differentiates itself through a commitment to energy efficiency and carbon reduction technologies. It is a key player in the Stargate Project, a $500 billion AI infrastructure investment led by OpenAI, Oracle, and MGX. Ownership Crusoe is a venture-based startup. Owners include founders, employees, and a large pool of venture capital firms and individual investors. Venture capital investors include Founders Fund, Mubadala, Fidelity, Long Journey Ventures, Nvidia, Ribbit Capital, Valor Equity Partners, G2 Venture Partners, Atreides Management, Bain Capital, CMT Digital, Castle Island Ventures, DRW Venture Capital, Engine No. 1, Exor Ventures, Felicis, Fifth Down Capital, Lowercarbon Capital, MCJ, Mitsui & Company, Polychain Capital, Rosemoor Capital, Winklevoss Capital, Zigg Capital, Valor Equity Partners, KCK Group, and Wicklow Capital.     "content": "AI data center startup Fluidstack in talks for $1B round at $18B valuation months after hitting $7.5B, says report | TechCrunch \u2013:\u2013:\u2013:\u2013 The first StrictlyVC of 2026 hits SF on April 30. Close TechCrunch Desktop Logo TechCrunch Mobile Logo Latest Startups Venture Apple Security AI Apps Events Podcasts Newsletters Search Submit Site Search Toggle Mega Menu Toggle Topics Latest AI Amazon Apps Biotech & Health Climate Cloud Computing Commerce Crypto Enterprise EVs Fintech Fundraising Gadgets Gaming Google Government & Policy Hardware Instagram Layoffs Media & Entertainment Meta Microsoft Privacy Robotics Security Social Space Startups TikTok Transportation Venture More from TechCrunch Staff Events Startup Battlefield StrictlyVC Newsletters Podcasts Videos Partner Content TechCrunch Brand Studio Crunchboard Contact Us Image Credits: TechCrunch / AI generated Startups AI data center startup Fluidstack in talks for $1B round at $18B valuation months after hitting $7.5B, says report Julie Bort 2:33 PM PDT \u00b7 April 14, 2026 Fluidstack, a startup that builds specialized data centers for AI companies, is in talks to raise a $1 billion round at an $18 billion valuation, potentially led by Jane Street, Bloomberg reports. Should this deal come to fruition, it would more than double Fluidstack\u2019s valuation in a matter of months. In December, the company was reportedly raising around $700 million at a $7.5 billion valuation, sources told Bloomberg at the time, although it didn\u2019t formally announce the close of that round. That round was said to be led by Situational Awareness, an AGI-focused fund founded by former OpenAI researcher Leopold Aschenbrenner, and backed by Stripe\u2019s Collison brothers, former GitHub CEO Nat Friedman, and the AI investor and entrepreneur Daniel Gross. Talks were apparently still ongoing for this round in February, at least with Google, which was considering kicking in $100 million to the round, The Wall Street Journal reported. There\u2019s good reason for the hype over Fluidstack. In November, Anthropic announced that it had signed a $50 billion deal with the startup to build data centers custom-designed for its needs in Texas and New York. Unlike hyperscalers like AWS, which serve all kinds of computing needs, Fluidstack\u2019s infrastructure is built specifically for AI. The deal was a huge vote of confidence for Fluidstack, a company that was relatively unknown in the U.S. Anthropic primarily uses AWS and Google Cloud to serve Claude (though it also has a partnership with Microsoft to supply Claude to that software giant\u2019s customers). But just like rival OpenAI, Anthropic is growing so fast that it needs more capacity, and this deal gives Anthropic more control over its own cloud infrastructure. This partnership is so significant to the startup that Fluidstack \u2014 which was spun out of Oxford and had been a rising star in Europe\u2019s AI scene \u2014 relocated its headquarters from the U.K. to New York. The proposed valuation would more than double Fluidstack\u2019s previous $7.5bn valuation earlier this year, underscoring the rapid repricing of companies positioned at the centre of artificial intelligence infrastructure. Fluidstack specialises in building and operating large-scale computing infrastructure, and recently secured a $50bn agreement with Anthropic to develop custom data centres, further strengthening its position in the market.     "content": "FluidStack is in talks to raise $1B at an $18B valuation Skip to content Toggle Navigation News Events TNW Conference June 19 & 20, 2025 All events Spaces Programs Newsletters Partner with us Jobs Contact News Latest Deep tech Sustainability Ecosystems Data and security Fintech and ecommerce Future of work Conference media hub More Startups and technology Investors and funding Government and policy Corporates and innovation Podcast Investors and funding FluidStack is in talks to raise $1B at an $18B valuation April 15, 2026 - 7:14 am Jane Street and Situational Awareness are in discussions to co-lead the round, Bloomberg reports. The Oxford-founded neocloud, which relocated from the UK to the US after landing a $50 billion data centre partnership with Anthropic, has grown its revenue from $1.8M in 2022 to $66.2M in 2024. FluidStack , the AI data centre startup that builds compute infrastructure specifically for AI companies, is in talks to raise approximately $1 billion at a target valuation of $18 billion, Bloomberg reported on Tuesday , citing people briefed on the matter. Trading firm Jane Street and Situational Awareness, an AGI-focused fund founded by former OpenAI researcher Leopold Aschenbrenner and backed by the Collison brothers, former GitHub CEO Nat Friedman, and investor Daniel Gross, are in discussions to co-lead the round. Morgan Stanley is advising on the investment. The $18 billion target valuation would represent a more than doubling of the company\u2019s worth in a matter of months. In December 2025, Bloomberg reported that FluidStack was in discussions to raise around $700 million at a $7.5 billion valuation, in a round said to be led by Situational Awareness; that round did not formally close or receive public confirmation. TNW City Coworking space - Where your best work happens A workspace designed for growth, collaboration, and endless networking opportunities in the heart of tech. Book a tour now The new talks suggest the company\u2019s negotiating position has strengthened considerably since then, almost certainly because of the $50 billion data centre partnership it signed with Anthropic in November 2025. That Anthropic deal , to build custom facilities in Texas and New York , designed specifically to maximise efficiency for Anthropic\u2019s Claude AI workloads, with sites coming online throughout 2026, was the largest infrastructure commitment Anthropic had made independently from cloud providers such as AWS and Google. It represented a defining moment for FluidStack, an Oxford-founded neocloud that had been a notable but relatively unknown presence in European AI infrastructure before the deal. Following its signing, the company relocated its headquarters from the UK to the United States. FluidStack was founded in 2017 as a spin-off from Oxford University and pivoted aggressively towards AI compute infrastructure following the release of ChatGPT in late 2022. Revenue grew from $1.8 million in 2022 to $66.2 million in 2024 as the company built out capacity for AI customers. It manages more than 100,000 GPUs and counts Anthropic, Meta, Poolside, Black Forest Labs, and France\u2019s Mistral among its customers. A $200 million Series A"

Advertisement --> Fluidstack, a startup building specialized data centers for AI companies, is negotiating a $1 billion funding round at an $18 billion valuation, according to reports citing sources familiar with the discussions. Recent Valuation Jump Fluidstack's valuation has risen sharply from $7.5 billion in December 2025, when it was in talks to raise about $700 million in a round led by Situational Awareness, a fund founded by former OpenAI researcher Leopold Aschenbrenner and backed by Stripe co-founders Patrick and John Collison, former GitHub CEO Nat Friedman, and investor Daniel Gross[2][4]. Talks for that round continued into February 2026, with Google considering a $100 million investment, according to The Wall Street Journal[4]. The current $1 billion round at $18 billion reflects accelerated investor interest amid surging AI infrastructure demand[1][5]. Key Investors and Advisors Trading firm Jane Street and Situational Awareness are in discussions to co-lead the new round, with Morgan Stanley providing advisory services[3][4]. Situational Awareness's prior involvement underscores continued backing from prominent AI-focused investors[2][3]. Anthropic Deal Drives Momentum Fluidstack's growth is tied to a November 2025 agreement with Anthropic for $50 billion in custom data centers in Texas and New York, though upfront cash is lower[4]. Unlike general cloud providers such as AWS, Fluidstack designs infrastructure optimized for AI training and inference workloads, offering AI firms greater control over costs and performance[3][4]. AI Infrastructure Valuation Premium Fluidstack's rapid valuation increase from $7.5 billion to $18 billion in months highlights the premium investors place on specialized AI infrastructure amid capacity constraints at major hyperscalers[1][3]. The $50 billion Anthropic commitment signals strong demand from leading AI labs for tailored data centers that address unique workload needs, such as high-performance computing for model training[4]. This positions Fluidstack as a key player in a market where general-purpose providers struggle to meet customization and speed requirements[3]. Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 7, 2026 Fluidstack: UK Start-Up Hits $7B Valuation After US Pivot A data centre company founded in London has reached a valuation of seven billion dollars following a substantial capital raise. According to a report from Yahoo Finance, the firm, Fluidstack , secured over seven hundred and fifty million dollars in funding, with the final portion secured last month. The start-up was established in 2017 by Gary Wu, Cesar Maklary, and James Cox. It is now engaged in an extensive initiative to construct artificial intelligence infrastructure facilities in the United States. Late last year, the company announced it would move its headquarters from London to New York, a relocation intended to position it nearer to major American clients. This move is expected to generate over one thousand employment positions in New York. As part of its strategic pivot to the US market, the company reportedly withdrew from a planned data centre project in France. This facility had been a central component of the French government's artificial intelligence policy. The recent funding round attracted new investors, including an artificial intelligence hedge fund named Situational Awareness. This fund was founded by a former employee of OpenAI and manages approximately five and a half billion dollars in assets. Fluidstack's existing financial support includes a significant guarantee from Google , covering lease obligations for its data centres. Discussions are also underway regarding Google potentially acquiring an ownership interest in the start-up. Furthermore, Fluidstack is collaborating with the developer of the Claude artificial intelligence system on a project to develop data facilities in New York and Texas.     "content": "Google-affiliated data centre seeks jumbo funding via junk bonds | IDNFinancials Search English English Indonesian \u65e5\u672c\u8a9e Login Login Home Companies News Videos Tools Market Hints Bonds Call People Net Foreign Stake Monitor 1%+ Valuable Stock Foreign Exchange Rates Business Info Subscribe Select Language! English English Indonesian \u65e5\u672c\u8a9e IDR / USD: ---- As of: More Google-affiliated data centre seeks jumbo funding via junk bonds ZH DK Zetta Hannany, Dhika Priambodo 16 April 2026, 08:48 WIB ZH Zetta Hannany Reporter DK Dhika Priambodo Reporter \u00d7 WhatsApp Channel A Google-linked data centre is seeking USD 5.7 billion via junk bonds to expand AI infrastructure. WASHINGTON \u2013 A data centre affiliated with Alphabet Inc. through its Google unit is exploring a USD 5.7 billion fundraising through high-yield (junk) bond issuance. If realised, the transaction would mark the largest financing deal for large-scale artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure expansion. As reported by Bloomberg , Morgan Stanley has begun marketing the bonds on Wednesday and is expected to price the deal in the near term, according to sources familiar with the matter. Proceeds from the issuance will be used to build two data centres in Sullivan County, Indiana, which will later be leased to cloud computing start-up Fluidstack Ltd. with support from Google. Read Also: Garuda revenue up 5.36% in early 2026, losses narrow The rapid acceleration of AI development has led to significant shortages in data centre capacity, GPU chips and access to high-speed electricity. To finance these needs, companies are increasingly turning to various debt instruments, ranging from high-yield bonds to project financing. At USD 5.7 billion, the issuance would be the largest US dollar-denominated junk bond tied to data centre financing, testing investor appetite for the sector, while also marking the biggest transaction led by a single Wall Street institution, Morgan Stanley. The deal could also surpass a previous record, likewise led by Morgan Stanley, involving a high-yield bond issuance for crypto miner TeraWulf Inc., which is also backed by Google. The five-year bonds will be issued by Meridian Arc HoldCo LLC, a joint venture between Next Frontier LLC and Fluidstack. Fluidstack focuses on high-performance computing-based data centre development, a sector experiencing rapid growth alongside rising AI demand. Recently, Fluidstack also announced a USD 50 billion partnership with Anthropic to build data centres dedicated to large language model development. In addition, the company is expanding the use of data centre facilities operated by TeraWulf in New York, while Google has increased both its financing support and equity stake in TeraWulf to around 14%.
